ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u146957	Dear Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament, allow me to briefly present the Final Proposal for the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Road Safety Act. The government of the Republic of Croatia proposes this law so as to amend the Road Safety Act in order to raise the level of safety on Croatian roads, to reduce the number of killed, injured and material damages. We believe that the number of people killed on our roads is still extremely high despite all the efforts that are being made to reduce it in the last year in the Republic of Croatia was 7.7 people killed to 100,000 people while the EU average of 5 people killed to 100,000, and it is certainly necessary to pursue as few as possible in those countries that have the least dead in the EU, so about 3 to 100,000 people such as Sweden, Ireland, the Netherlands, Denmark and the UK. The Government of the Republic of Croatia finds by this final proposal that the current criminal sanctions, henceforth, have not yielded sufficient results to deter the perpetrator from repeating illegal behaviour and that those sanctions need to be tightened. We are particularly emphasising the problem of relapses, so the repetitions that we believe so far have not been sanctioned enough yet should give greater emphasis on the punishment of relapsers because they are both the worst and most severe traffic offences that have the most devastating consequences. We see that the behavior of a large number of drivers, it has been established and different surveys still do not, does not go towards road safety. In particular, we stress the improper use of mobile devices during driving, the non-binding of the safety belt, the illegal transport of children in vehicles. We believe that these amendments and amendments to the law will help reduce the number of traffic accidents and casualties in them, that we will draw closer to the goal set out in the National Road Safety Programme to cut the number of victims by the end of 2020 compared to 10 years earlier. We have come close but we have not yet reached that goal and we will work and we will take all measures and activities to get close to that goal and reduce the number of victims. We believe that, in addition to education and prevention, which should certainly be the first, but also the joint and coordinated work of all stakeholders affecting road safety, that these changes will affect the awareness of traffic participants about the need for strict compliance with traffic regulations and deter them from doing the most serious traffic offences again. Some new measures are also introduced, such as the temporary confiscation of vehicles to drivers who commit the most serious traffic offences, and thus the means of committing an offence is taken away and they are prevented from taking part in the traffic. Similarly, for the most serious infringements, it is proposed to prescribe the minimum duration of the protection measure for the prohibition of the management of motor vehicle dependence on, depending on the number of repeated infringements within the range prescribed by law or law, so within 3 years. We intend to simplify the verification of the number of negative misdemeanor points, safeguards and validity of the driver's license via the MUP website. More precisely, we define the provisions relating to the suspension of driving licences for drivers who have collected 9 or 12 negative misdemeanor points. The goal is also to achieve maximum in our plans, and that is 98% of the use of the safety belt in drivers and passengers in vehicles according to the national programme I mentioned earlier. Also, since the knowledge and skills of motor vehicle management on the roads come from laying down, from learning in auto school and passing exams, the aim is to increase the quality, transparency and objectivity in the process of passing tests from the scientific course of car management. Once again, I will repeat those 8 most serious violations that are proposed to increase fines, and these are those violations which, by doing so, result in the most severe accidents and the most severe consequences. So with the proposal of this law, with current 5 to 15 thousand kunas, penalties are increased to 10 to 20 thousand kuna for these 8 serious violations, which is driving in the opposite or forbidden direction of the highway, the fast road or the road intended exclusively for the transport of motor vehicles. Last year it was just, I'll just say, so number 39, that sounds like a small number of violations, but six people died in them. It's certainly one of the most difficult shops in the world. The next violation that will be sanctioned by this fine is an overrun of the permitted speed of movement in a populated place by more than 50km per hour. These violations last year were reported in 1243. And the reason, they were the reason for a total of 22% of traffic accidents. In those accidents, 158 people were killed or 49% of all died. The next offence sanctioned by these most severe penalties is the intentional passage through red light, i.e. passing through light without reduction, red light without reducing speed, increasing speed or passing through two or more red lights. Such violations in the past year have been reported by nearly 5,000 people, and as a result of such violations, 12 people were killed. Then this sanction is also provided for refusing to submit an examination to check whether there is alcohol or drugs in the body, so rejection of alcohol abuse, for example, and then driving under the influence of alcohol above 1.5 prominence, or drug-induced driving. Then drive the vehicle before the right to drive. In the last year, 8 thousand 258 of these violations or 1.1 were reported. So over 1% of the people who committed all the crimes were, they were persons who had no right to drive at all. Then vehicle management while driver's license has been suspended due to collected negative misdemeanor points. It is a 7-8, 8th vehicle management offence while the driver's license is withdrawn, while the driver is excluded from traffic or has been granted a temporary withdrawal of the driver's licence or a safeguard measure. Therefore, except for these most difficult works in transport, fines are increased significantly and for a violation of the use of the safety belt during the ride from the current 500 to 100 kuna. Last year, a total of 85,000 464 violations or 11.8% of the total number of violations were found and 31,000 770 were recorded in the first 5 months of this year. So it's by frequency a second offense after a speeding breach. It is particularly risky to carry children in a vehicle, and the proposal is to sanction the offence now with 1000 kuna, so far it has been a fine of 500 kuna. Three children were killed last year in traffic and 109 were seriously injured. We feel that we need to influence parents or parents who are responsible for children to be carried more responsibly in the vehicle. And at the end of these frequent misdemeanors, I would single out the improper use of a cell phone while driving. The penalty, therefore, is increasing from 500 to 1500 kuna. Last year, a total of 41 000 such violations or 5.7% of the total number of violations were found. We see that more and more people use their cell phone to talk and to check some apps and cause obvious use of mobile phones and cause traffic accidents that could certainly have been avoided in some places where in the flat areas of the road inexplicable crossings to the other side and so on. It is difficult to determine in any case whether the cell phone is the reason but if the exception is some kind of heart attack or stroke, then some difficult medical condition that could have occurred at that time obviously that what points to another offense is certainly an improper use of the cell phone while driving. In short, I have also stated the others, so the regulation of certain other measures such as safeguard measures, the increase of negative misdemeanor points in a certain range for a particular offence. I would point out here that exactly the maximum number of negative points for a particular offense, which is 6, says for the eight most severe violations I have pointed out at the outset. In short, I would like to make the difference between the first, the bill and the final bill. The debate that was held in this home amended Article 1 of this Law, so where is the definition of fast roads. Then, on the proposal of the Committee on Internal Policy, as well as individual representatives in the debate at the plenary session, Article 9 which prescribes a violation of the speed out of the settlement by more than 50 km has been amended. In the final draft of the law, only fines ranging from 5 to 15,000 are prescribed, so this remains from the bill, and is deleted and cancelled from the alternative rule of imprisonment for up to 60 days. So it's not in the final bill. At the proposal of the Legislative Committee, nomotechnical arrangements were carried out in Articles 18, 24, 38 and 55. On the proposal of the National Security Council Office, it is prescribed that vehicles of the Security Intelligence Agency shall be exempted from the provisions of Article 150 of this Act. In Article 40, the provision of information on persons who were driving a vehicle at the time of the infringement is specified if it was not driven by its owner, in order to reduce certain manipulations and to report that the vehicle is at that time or that day more persons, it is written about the corruption that will prevent such manipulations and, all in accordance with the case-law we wanted to translate into this law. It shall also be complemented in that Article 40 that the perpetrator of the offence, other than the costs of keeping the vehicle, shall bear the costs of moving the withdrawn vehicle. Certain remarks made by the working authorities and representatives could not be accepted, primarily those aimed at reducing the prescribed sanctions, so the applicant remains at the proposed sanctions or their level because we hold that in multiple offences, in the case of the most serious offences where there must be awareness that the offence seems to be appropriate and sanctioned. It was also not possible to accept the objection of the Committee on Internal Policy and National Security to introduce the possibility of giving two or more warnings prior to the sentence, in view of the provisions of the Criminal Code which prescribes that the warning is pronounced if it is found that an offence has been committed which has been imposed in a fine of up to 1000 kuna or in some other cases, so that when the offence is not caused by interference or endangerment of other traffic participants or if the offence has been particularly of an easy nature and the perpetrator has not previously committed similar offences and it can be expected that the statement of oral or written warning will achieve the purpose of punishment. We could not accept the proposals of the Committee on Internal Policy and National Security and the Committee on Maritime Affairs, Transport and Links regarding the reduction of age criteria for driving instructors, so they asked that driving instructors may be persons less than 24 years old because it is a training of other persons on driving a vehicle where a certain experience is needed in driving a particular life experience and given the other changes in this law that have been implemented, which were adopted at the end of 2017 due to compliance with the European Services Directive currently and according to the Croatian Autoclub data we have enough instructors and here we do not see a problem that we need to lower the age limit for driving instructors. In addition to these sanctions most discussed when this law is amended, and in the name of the promoter, I can say that it was by no means the aim of sanctions is merely punishment, but indeed a warning to those most hard-core perpetrators of the offence, and this is not about any violations, but about violations that very often lead to death, suffering and serious injuries, so that all other measures and actions, warnings and education did not bear fruit, so we consider it necessary for such persons to impose more severe sanctions which we believe will deter them from committing the most serious traffic offences. But the MUP, in cooperation with other bodies, works daily and persistently on preventive activities and police believe that it should act proactively to combat negative trends in road safety throughout the year, systematically work on preventive activities primarily to target the most vulnerable categories of persons and road users such as children, young drivers, pedestrians, cyclists, motorcycle drivers and elderly people. All of you are familiar with our actions Respect our signs that has become an irreplaceable part of the start of each school year in primary schools, traffic teacher is one web application for obtaining a traffic culture that has become part of civil courtesy among primary and high school students. With the Rod Association that we work with to protect children through car seats always and without exception we try to educate parents to use the car seat properly and increase the safety of their children in traffic. In collaboration with road companies, cities, municipalities and counties, many dangerous places have been remedied in the last 15 years, nearly 170 such places, so in those places where traffic accidents have been frequented and people have been injured, no more traffic accidents have been reported since the recovery in those places. Of course, in order to be able to carry out quality road safety surveillance, the police need both more modern and better equipment and devices, than speed control devices over enough vehicles, other devices such as alcometers, cameras, drug testicles, motorcycle uniforms and helmets, reflecting vests, ropes, measuring carts, etc., all those equipment used by traffic police in road safety surveillance. In this sense, a certain number of fixed vehicle speed control devices currently in place at 28, and there are 62 cabinets and housings, so there are currently 59 fixed devices and 22 housings that will be placed in the area of all police administrations and stations, currently these fixed, stationary cameras are located in the 6 police administrations of Zagrebačka, Split-Dalmatia, Primorje-Gorski, Osijek-Baranja, Karlovak and Varazdinska. There are also 76 manual devices for measuring vehicle speed movements at a further stage. All of our activities, our activities, can't deliver results unless they include the broadest circle of institutions, as well as associations and individuals. It is especially important that when the driver finishes his education and education for the driver, when he passes the driver's exam to the maximum possible extent that he is trained for self-manage of the vehicle, so the quality of the work of the car school is very important to us, the safety of the vehicle through the technical examinations that we hold fitly organized in the Republic of Croatia, so we should continue to work hard to have as high-quality correct vehicles as possible, that we have better roads, but all this is expensive if an individual when he sits behind the wheel and is involved in the traffic not mentally prepared and healthy and aware of the dangers he carries. Although we continue to be deeply affected by data that the police almost daily publishes about the number of alcoholic drivers for the driver of their vehicle, these numbers are being reduced, but obviously not enough to significantly affect road safety. Therefore, education, but when education and prevention do not result in a certain number of persons, it is certainly a smaller number of people, a smaller number of drivers, it is also necessary to prescribe appropriate sanctions and then sanctions in fast-track procedures and apply in a legal manner. Therefore, other than the MUP and the criminal courts to which such cases occur, they should be carried out within a specified period of time and levelen their practice that whenever they come with a certain accusation that they know that they will be adequately punished in accordance with the severity of the infringement they have committed. I can say with some satisfaction, however, that the results of road safety in this year are better than in the same period last year, in the first six months and 10 days we have 128 deaths, unfortunately, but that is 29 people less than on the same day last year or about 18%. It's one shift, but it's absolutely not enough to make our roads safe and not be a place where irresponsible individuals will live and endanger other people's lives. As representatives of the Croatian Parliament, I invite you to bring our proposals to the best possible law. I repeat again is not the goal of sanctioning people, no one wants to, no government wants to resent people, but if the choice between neglect and reduction is the number of deaths, this government has decided to put an end to irresponsible individuals and reduce the number of victims on Croatian highways. Here on behalf of the government, I ask you to help us with that. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147007	The Honorable Vice President of HS, the Honorable Secretary of State and Chief. Dear colleagues and fellow MPs. Almost every day, unfortunately, we meet with numerous signs in black media chronicles from all parts of Croatia concerning road accidents. Such situations are evidence of poor transport culture in Croatian society, knowingly non-compliance with the rules, mostly perceptions that these regulations do not concern individuals and that they are above them, that it is easier to pay for a symbolic punishment than to change the way they drive and their own habits. By observing the proposed amendments and additions to the law at first sight, it is noticeably according to which these changes are primarily directed and are primarily directed towards relapsers, people who often violate road safety regulations in the most difficult ways, thereby bringing all other road users to death. There is a visible disproportionateity between the number of such offenders and the number of most serious forms of infringements committed in this regard, which is precisely a sign that the legal changes must be directed at them. If they can't and don't want to change their own bad habits and traffic failures, that's where the state and these amendments and the additions to the law are going to be involved. These changes and are complemented by a response to the multiannual requirements, not only of traffic and security experts, but also of all citizens seeking stricter sanctioning of the most serious traffic offences. These measures introduced by these amendments and amendments are repressive, but their purpose is not repressive nor is the aim of these changes filling the budget as some want to say already the purpose of both general and special preventive. What else do we meet regularly? So driving during the duration of the ban on driving, non-use of the safety belt, illegal transport of children in vehicles, driving in an alcoholic state as well as regular and frequent use of mobile devices during driving, as shown by one of the biggest distractions in motor vehicle management and thus one of the biggest causes of traffic accidents in Croatia today. According to a research carried out as part of a project co-financed by the National Road Safety Programme, a day without a mobile, as many as 91% of drivers telephone during driving, and unfortunately, 33% of cyclists seem almost amazing. What is important to point out, so after that study 55% of drivers showed a willingness to change such a habit when the penalties planned would be more severe. The fact that we have to fight such habits that cause a significantly reduced concentration enables a large increase in the number of traffic accidents. The aim is indeed to adjust the amount of penalties to the severity of these violations, and these consequences are great. The penalty so far for using a cell phone, so it's proposed in a triple amount, I personally think it's too large, to be enough in double amount and to achieve a purpose and to safely reduce the use of a cell phone. The proposed changes and additions introduce a differentiation within the breach of provisions concerning the passage through red light at a traffic light or proposed that it will be more difficult to sanction the passage through a red light that includes, above all, the intention, as well as the passing without slowing down, or by speeding or passing through two or more red lights, while the lesser punishment will be to pass through a red light which is obviously a product of carelessness and did not lead to more serious consequences. The introduction of such a subjective criterion in measuring the penalty for this serious offence is precisely showing the general preventive purpose of these amendments and additions and that the aim is not equal to punishing everyone, but above all changing bad habits and particularly punishing rude negligence, or in the worst cases and intentions in committing this serious offence. Another problem with which this proposal is being amended and the law supplemented is driving in an alcoholic state or driving under the influence of an opiate that is one of the main causes of the most severe traffic accidents with the most severe consequences. This is particularly decisive to be put to the end, and the proposed changes are exactly going in this direction. What most looks at is the preventive purpose of the proposed changes and the addition is to increase the duration of the protection measures for the prohibition of the management of the motor vehicle. Two categories have also been introduced around the duration of that protection measure in the case of the most serious offences such as traffic on the freeway and on the fast road, when passing through red light or driving under the influence of drugs and alcohol. According to this proposal, if a second infringement is committed, the duration of the safeguard measure shall be prescribed within at least 6 months, while for the third and next time a period and longer shall be prescribed, i.e. from the shortest of 12 months. Also included in the law is the maximum amount of protection measures taken under the Criminal Code, which is even 3.g. bans on the management of motor vehicles, for minor infringements, the duration is set at a lower amount. The proposed provisions also introduce a new system of measuring negative points for offenders of road safety provisions, which is precisely aimed at frequent repeaters of the most serious traffic offences. This system will have a significant impact on the awareness of such offenders because in the event of frequent infringements, they can lead to the final withdrawal of the driver’s licence and the re-suspending of the traffic and driving exams. So far, the maximum number of points for each offense has been 3 and with this proposal, the number doubles and goes to 6 negative points. A vehicle owned or owned by an irresponsible person, persons who in the most difficult ways are in breach of the rules on road safety indeed ceases to be only a means of transport, but becomes a weapon, a weapon that directly endangers all road users. It is precisely because this proposal provides for a special measure to amend and supplement, which is a measure of confiscation of the vehicle which was the means of committing the offence. Two things consider the importance of this provision. First, in the motor vehicle in which such serious offences have been committed, fully correct treatment, arms treatment and general dangerous substances are given which directly threatens the safety of all road users in the hands of the wrong person. And secondly, this is encouraged and appealed to a kind of responsibility of the owner of the motor vehicle which is a bit unfortunately and forgotten, which is the responsibility of thinking to whom this vehicle is given for use, whether the person receiving the vehicle is responsible enough and capable of driving. Each owner of the vehicle is obliged to know who he gives his vehicle to and whom he trusts to use and to examine whether that person has the necessary minimum knowledge and ability at that time so that he does not indeed make a weapon and endanger the participants in the vehicle. This is especially important for rent-a-cars, so with this ability to see and in the state of the code, in the traffic sense of the driver's license and everything else. This is a temporary suspension of the right of ownership of a short-term motor vehicle to a court decision which stipulates that it must take it within 15 days and refers to the driver who has already been fined twice and commits one of the most serious offences mentioned above, thereby not jeopardising anyone's private ownership of the motor vehicle, but preventing further threats to the public safety and safety of the life of the participants in the transport. I also particularly commend the proposed provision that will record driving exams with video and audio records. Such a measure whose implementation is very simple and cheap will also raise the level of transparency of this important exam, while at the same time certainly raising the level of its quality, and certainly there will be a leveling of criteria in the surveyors. This is not only for the benefit of the students of the car school, but also for the benefit of the interrogators and car schools. The purpose of this provision shall be reflected in the quality monitoring of the performance of the driving test, the supervision of the authorised investigator, and in the final analysis and evaluation of the driving test process. According to current practice, it is impossible to verify the candidates who failed to satisfy the results of the exams, and the introduction of audio visual recording will allow the identification of relevant facts during the driving test. Recording would increase the transparency of laying and quality as well as the ability to monitor. It is also important to stress that it is not intended for that recording to collect personal data and that it cannot be used for other purposes. The proposed provisions of the MUP will also allow the rapid electronic verification of the driver’s driver’s license status through their websites as well as the number of negative points and current penalties and safeguards. This proposal will enable each carrier to rent and the car company and others, to check the driver to which the vehicle is given for use and thus to ensure that all unwanted consequences are ensured and the other parties shall disable the driver in the illegal management of the motor vehicle. These measures are not the end of achieving the objectives of safer road traffic and a significant reduction in the number of victims on traffic accidents, but only the beginning. These measures should further encourage all competent authorities to carry out even more preventive actions that will try to raise awareness of drivers in a milder way. As I have stated, it is not the goal of filling the budget but of preserving life as the greatest social value. All these measures would reduce the number of accidents on Croatian roads and thus reduce the number of fatal accidents. We are obliged to take all steps and do as much as possible in changing the bad habits of individual drivers, raising transport culture and protecting the lives of transport participants and the proposed amendments to this law represent a quality and decisive step forward in this direction, and in this sense I support the adoption of this law.

ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147181	Thank you very much. Dear ladies and gentlemen, the representatives, chairing both of these laws, which will be discussed today, were thoroughly processed in 2015 and arranged by them among other things, the Law on Jobs and Activities, the conduct of professional affairs of spatial planning, design or professional supervision of construction, the operation of construction, the operation of jobs and activities, the management of the project of construction, and the operation of the business and the activities of studies and previous research. This law also provides for the provision of services in the capacity of responsible persons under the task of professions, the obligation to pass an expert examination, responsible persons in spatial planning and construction, professional training and conditions for foreign persons performing the activities of spatial planning and construction in the Republic of Croatia on a temporary or occasional basis or on a permanent basis. The purpose of the adoption of this Law on jobs and activities of spatial planning and construction is to administratively relieve professional business services, which is part of the government's current economic policy. It shall be implemented in accordance with the measures laid down in the Administrative Discharge Action Plan 2019, which was adopted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia in January '19. A year. Namely, the measurement of administrative burdens for construction engineers or workers by applying the standard cost methodology has shown a significant administrative discharge for cca 5,500 chamber members who would replace mandatory membership in the chamber with an optional register in the chamber directory. At the average price of the annual membership fee, savings from cca 10 million kuna per year were estimated, and this cost was partly poured over to the employer. According to the OCD methodology, the Croatian market for professional services is at least 1/3 more regulated than the European average. The World Bank estimates the potential for growth in the productivity of the Croatian economy to about 5% if a certain level of reduction in excessive regulation is implemented. The European Commission's indicator of the restrictive nature of professions also shows that the Republic of Croatia has at least a 10% higher overall level of regulation of professions than the EU average. Furthermore, regarding the commitment made under the Treaty of Accession of the Republic of Croatia, the government is on 10 May 18th. adopt a strategy for the introduction of the EUR as an official currency in the Republic of Croatia. Joining the European EREM2 Course Mechanism and the EU Banking Union is one of the steps that confirms the consistency of the implementation of reforms that contribute to economic growth and already shows positive results. The following recognized the need for mandatory membership in the Croatian Chamber of Architects, the Croatian Chamber of Engineering, the Chamber of Engineering Engineers, the Croatian Chamber of Electrical Engineers for Construction Engineers or Work Managers to be replaced by an optional register in the chamber directory. Engineers of construction sites or workers according to the law governing construction are not involved in construction because they do not carry out these tasks professionally and economically independently, e.g. they do not buy construction material and no contract on construction has been signed, and therefore cannot take responsibility for the performance of the contract. Since engineers of construction sites or workers like all other performers are responsible only to the employer, and not to the investor, professional insurance is not needed, and in this context cannot even be talked about the need to edit this profession as a regulated profession. Also, the emphasis so far on the special benefits for chamber members in the form of favourable or free access to Croatian norm repositories is not a decisive factor for these two professions because it is generally known that they do not need more frequent access to norms. According to the Croatian Institute for Standards, the Croatian Standards repository uses only 22% of total chamber membership, and it is known which professions are required to regularly monitor norms, designers and supervisory engineers, and it is evident that they are not even more users of the said service. As indicated above, by equating the right to perform works of construction engineers or workers registered in the chamber and those not entered in the chamber, the professional name as well as its formal supplement authorised becomes unnecessary. The right to use a professional name is the right to be bound by applicable legal provisions by the law of the prescribed membership in the chamber. Therefore, the need for further enforcement of the right to an professional name will cease, and this right will be exercised through the fulfilment of the law of the prescribed conditions for the performance of the work for the benefit of all construction engineers or workers who are partially deregulationd i.e. This amendment corrects the excessive regulation introduced in 2015. Because until then construction engineers and workers were not mandatory members of the chamber. Therefore, the mechanisms which existed until 2015. remain in place with the additional possibility of membership if an individual engineer would have been able to do so in an opportune manner. These two engineering professions must continue to meet the same essential and legal requirements of professional and professional competence, and on the basis of these essential rather than formal administrative assumptions, they exercise the right to work. Furthermore, these legislative proposals are subject to the transposition of the provisions of the Directive on the recognition of professional qualifications, and the Directive on the recognition of professional qualifications and the Regulation on administrative cooperation through the information system of the internal market, the applicable law provides that in the first provision of services and requirements for the one-year exercise of professions over the past 10 years. the excluded applicants for the Member States whose profession is regulated but not the applicants from the Contracting State of the European Economic Area where education, professional training, training leading to that profession, is regulated, which is proposed by this law to be corrected. It is also not pointed out by the applicable law that the earlier verification of qualifications excludes the professions to which automatic recognition applies, and therefore this law also makes it more clear. The relevant law stipulates that experts involved in the activities of spatial planning and construction belong to the security sector and is therefore required of them at the time of the first provision of the service for impunity even though the Republic of Croatia does not require their own citizens to do so. The relevant law lays down a national requirement for insurance against professional liability in the event of temporary or occasional provision of services by a natural person in the territory of the Republic of Croatia, rather than providing the service provider with detailed information on any insurance or other means of personal or collective protection. It is not pointed out by the relevant law that a party may start providing its services immediately after a statement or delivery of complete documentation, and that the confirmation of the chamber is not an authorisation for the temporary or occasional provision of the service. Furthermore, the Chamber Act partially changes public powers and the framework of jurisdiction and a correction of expressions has been carried out in the part in which it has proved inadequate in the present application. The proposed laws therefore implement 3 essential elements, hence harmonisation, further compliance with the provisions of the Directive, reducing administrative barriers in the performance of operations in activity and spatial planning and construction and ensuring implementation of the action plan. In addition to the multiple benefits described, we note that the adoption of this law will not affect the reduction of the level of responsibility of professional persons and will ensure a certain administrative discharge in the performance of jobs and construction, and encourage chambers to raise professional responsibility. During a public debate in the preparation of this legislative proposal, the representatives of the engineering chambers expressed strong opposition using all available means, however, they failed to comment on their comments once. Such a reaction by the chamber representatives on the one hand is not surprising because it is a reform measure, and the reforms serve to regulate society and remove the anomalies that are obstacles to faster development, and to make the country more competitive in the international economic environment and more favourable to life for all and its citizens. On the other hand, as it is still about chambers that have their ethical codes, they surprise their unargumented loud protests against the proposed reform. As you know, the HS recently passed amendments to the Law on Construction and the Law on Spatial Planning, thus creating a more efficient, faster and cheaper system of issuing permits, and for the benefit of citizens and investors. These measures are already in place and we continue to develop both the digital system and additional red tape and parafiscal loads. This government therefore, responsible and intensively implemented reform direction for the prosperity of the Republic of Croatia and better tomorrow, all of our citizens. Thank you very much.

It should be pointed out that the right of a foreign professional person to carry out a job on a temporary and occasional basis in the Republic of Croatia without having passed an expert examination does not exempt those persons from the strict responsibility and professional control and knowledge of national law of the Republic of Croatia. ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147215	I would like to thank the Vice-President of the Croatian Parliament, the esteemed secretary of state, respected assistant ministers, respected colleagues and colleagues. Today we are discussing two draft laws, a draft law on amendments and additions to the Law on jobs and activities of spatial planning and construction and the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Chamber of Architects and Chambers of Engineers in Construction and Spatial Planning. For both laws, first reading. As regards the Proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Law on jobs and activities of spatial planning and construction, it should be pointed out that this law goes towards amending laws which are necessary not only for the implementation of an action plan for administrative redistribution of the economy from 2019 but also for the further arrangement of the area of professional affairs, spatial planning and construction, as well as for foreign persons performing the tasks and activities of spatial planning and construction in the Republic of Croatia. According to the action plan, high levels of administrative burdens are one of the main obstacles to the implementation of the competitiveness of the Croatian economy. So the purpose of adopting this law is the administrative dispensation of professional business services. In line with the measures set out in the action plan, mandatory membership is required in the Croatian Chamber of Architects, the Croatian Chamber of Engineers of Engineering, the Croatian Chamber of Engineering Engineers and the Croatian Chamber of Electrical Engineering for Construction Engineers or Work Managers are replaced by an optional entry into the chamber directory. As regards the implementation of the action plan for administrative redistribution of the economy for 2019, the suspension or simplification of administrative processes and obligations resulting in financial savings and the reduction of burdens on the part of the economy and particularly on the professions of construction engineers and workers. The law harmonises provisions governing the performance of spatial planning and construction professional activities with two directives on the recognition of spatial planning and construction and services and those on the internal market for the purpose of facilitating access to the single market for European economic space. Our market is also interesting for other builders, i.e. construction companies in the EU area, but also vice versa. We need free access to the economic operator market and, on the other hand, the necessity to achieve public interest. But without further ado, what we need to take care of and what we need to have is quality. The aim of this law is to regulate the conditions for performing spatial planning and construction activities and to ensure the right to provide services and the right of business established by Croatian economic operators and individuals within the European Economic Area in accordance with the rules for the cross-border provision of services and for the recognition of foreign qualifications. Foreign engineers of construction sites and workers from a Contracting State of the European Economic Area in which there is no mandatory membership in the chamber can already cross-borderly carry out construction operations in the Republic of Croatia on the basis of the conditions they meet in their country. That's where we don't have the national influence. This is regulated by the proposed legislative amendments to both legislative proposals. What is crucial is that, it will not affect the reduction of the level of responsibility of professionals. The relevant provisions of the law have not properly transposed the provisions of the two Directives mentioned and this is corrected by this law. With regard to the part of the provisions, the law in force which is prescribed to carry out professional activities is considered non-economic activity of general interest to be deleted. The conditions for the supply of data by an authorised natural person from the Contracting State of the European Economic Area shall also be prescribed to the appropriate chamber prior to the start of the occasional or occasional operation of spatial planning, design and professional supervision of the building of the responsible person. It shall also be prescribed to carry out the procedure for checking the foreign professional qualification in the submission of the first statement by the professional party and to ensure that the exemption is made from such verification by architects concerned with the autonomous recognition of professional qualifications. The certificate is no longer required to wait, but services can be provided immediately after the declaration of the chamber with the delivery of all the necessary documentation. All this shows that this legislative proposal reduces administrative barriers in the performance of spatial planning and construction activities and facilitates access to the European Economic Area market on cross-border provision of services. As regards the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Chamber of Architects and Chambers of Engineers in Construction and Spatial Planning, the purpose of the adoption of this Act but as far as we have heard and both of these laws is the administrative discharge of professional business services in line with the amendments made to the Action Plan for Administrative Discharge of the Economy. Therefore mandatory membership in the Croatian Chamber of Architects, the Croatian Chamber of Engineers of Construction, the Chamber of Engineers of Engineering and Chambers Engineers of Electrical Engineering, for builders of construction sites and workers, should be replaced by optional enrolment in the chamber directory. The measure represents only a continuation of the objective which is to introduce order into the system and remove parafiscal burdens and these memberships are exactly that. It should also be pointed out that this measure and opportunity to professional chambers to raise the level of quality of their services provided to their members. This is also an opportunity to motivate potential members to join, these amendments to the law allow voluntary membership and normal work under the same professional principles and responsibility. So what is most important, the adoption of this law will not affect the reduction of the level of responsibility. I'll leave my colleagues to Curai, thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147341	Dear MPs and representatives of the Croatian Parliament, we have before us the first strategic act of planning the national level in the field of sports that we have never had in almost 3 decades of our independence. On the basis of Article 2(2), The law on sport at the proposal of the Government of the Republic of Croatia brings it to the Croatian Parliament for an 8-year period. The national sport programme for 2019 – 2026. a comprehensive act on the development of sports in the Republic of Croatia and will be a foothold for the development of strategic documents of all national, regional and local sports organisations. Since sport is a multidisciplinary phenomenon in the drafting of this document, experts from various areas are involved. The Secretary of State had a professional working body whose members were experts in the field of kinesiology, economy and rights, and experts from other related areas such as sociology, medicine, construction and tourism were involved in the development of the National Sport Programme. All relevant stakeholders in the sport system from local sports communities to national sports associations and roof sports associations are also included. During the production of the document 7 workshops were held throughout Croatia, in Zagreb, Varazdin, Rijeka, Split, Zadar, Osijek and Bjelovar. The work included 395 sports workers from all counties, 182 personally attended workshops and their written proposals were actively involved in the process of drawing up this document. Workshops with all national sports alliances were held, for the purpose of analysis related to the recreation of citizens' activities a study was carried out on a representative sample of Croatian households with the aim of collecting data on the level and type of participation of all household members in various recreational activities. Also analyzed data from the Ministry of Finance related to budgetary allocations for sport at the level of municipalities, cities and counties were also collected as separate from the state budget. With the help of the Croatian Olympic Committee, the National Sports Federation, which is an education institution in the field of sport, collected data on the overall qualification structure of trainers currently in the system of sports while all roof sports associations provided information on their members and their sports results. During the period from 18 January to 18 February 2019, a public debate was held with an interested public during which 11 additional roundtables were held with stakeholders at local, regional and national level, with a total of about 250 participants. With the measures and activities envisaged, and in accordance with the subtitle of this document according to a healthy and active nation proud of sports successes, we will ensure the conditions for a balanced development of sport through the continuation of the achievement of the top sports results according to which the Republic of Croatia belongs to the world summit compared to the number of inhabitants, but we will ensure a significantly greater activity of the general population because in this segment, according to the analysis data, we are not standing well at the moment, and this is a kind of public health problem. In accordance with the shortcomings and opportunities of Croatian sport established in the analysis of the state of vision of the document: Croatia is a country of sport as a way of life for all its citizens and countries is recognizable for achieving top sports results. The mission of the document is a transparent and efficient management of the sport system so that all stakeholders continuously create preconditions for the development of all emerging organisational forms of sport. This document contains 6 general, 19 specific objectives which include 62 measures and 147 activities which will allow for the reduction of shortcomings in Croatian sport between 2019 and 2026 and the use of opportunities provided in all three organisational forms of sport, as well as the provision of the Sport Act or legal basis for this document. It is a competitive sport, sport in the system of education and sports recreation, or health-oriented physical activity. The budgetary allocations of the central state in these three years have increased by more than 100 million kuna and by more than 50% compared to 2016. In order to continue that trend, in order to achieve the desired effects of further investment, three specific objectives should be implemented and measures of the first general objective of ensuring conditions for the development of sport, which will ensure a more transparent and efficient functioning of the system through the coordination of public needs programmes in sport with the national sport programme and a clear definition of public needs in sport, the purpose of funds, the way in which they are allocated and their control. Clear criteria will be established for the evaluation of public needs programmes in sport, as well as for the categorisation of sports based on which sports of national interest will be determined, and to ensure a uniform distribution of resources and the development of all sports according to actual indicators of mass, results and other indicators relevant to each sport. It will also enable the creation of better quality conditions for further work, ossification and the achievement of top sports results. A system of separation coordination will be established for sport from different public sources, through the integration of different public administration bodies, which allocate funding for sport to create an effective rational system that will guarantee optimal use of funds. A recommendation will be made on the minimum allocation of funding budgets for sports at the level of units of local and regional self-government. A detailed analysis of the tax system will be made which has a direct or indirect impact on sport, both in the Republic of Croatia and in EU countries, and on the basis of documents on good practice, tax measures for sport will be defined. This will also take care of the VAT Act, the Law on profit tax, the Tax on Income Act, and the Law on Contributions. Despite the recognition of Croatian sport as an international value, data from the analysis that almost 70% of the population is inactive is alarming data and, as I have already mentioned, a serious public health problem. In addition, too few children are involved in regular sports activities through the education system, and Croatia has not used its many potentials to develop different forms of sports tourism. It is necessary to implement 3 specific objectives and measures of the other general objective, which is to improve health... physical exercise and increase the promotional values of sports, which will be continuously provided by means of co-financing of sports recreational activities at the state level and locally promote physical activity and encourage regular exercise of physical activity throughout life to improve the health of individuals, local communities and the entire nation. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] We will ensure the training of non-swimming children through the project Croatia swims in collaboration with the Croatian Swimming Federation, thus reducing the number of non-plyers in the Republic of Croatia. Swimming is one of the basic human needs, has a comprehensive impact on the human organism and the sense of sport, sports recreation or rehabilitation contributes greatly to the quality of life, and is particularly important as a prevention of drowning. There will be an increase in opportunities for health... physical exercise within the system of education through regular teaching, extracurricular activities because it is the best way to massage sports of children and young people. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] Conditions will be created for increasing the number of sports in the system of inter-class, inter-school competitions and will include students who do not participate in competitions in the activities of school sports companies in accordance with their needs and interests. The compensation for the work of kinesiologists for increased range of work in school sports companies will be provided, Chinese scientists will be further educated for working with people with disabilities, especially children and students with disabilities. People with disabilities will be integrated into the system of school and academic sport through the increase in sport programs offered to them and through quality sports staff insurance. One of the big problems of many athletes after the end of the sports career is the adjustment in a different way of life than those leading during their careers, and in the focus of the future development of sports in Croatia we will provide all the preconditions for a athlete to be satisfied with his position in society. In order to achieve this, it is necessary to implement 4 specific objectives and measures of the third general objective called improving the care of athletes, which will encourage the dual career of athletes through the provision of the necessary conditions for schooling during and after the completion of the sports career and will regulate the rights of athletes during schooling. Subsidising the training of current and former top athletes at high schools and attending adult education programmes for the acquisition of professional qualifications for sports work and the employment of top athletes will be ensured. A stable scholarship and care system will be provided during the career of top athletes by introducing a single state-level scholarship to athletes of first and second categories. It will ensure the settlement of mandatory contributions for top athletes of the first category. A working status will be introduced for professional athletes by contractual relationship modeling most of the EU countries, as well as improving the health care system for athletes. We will provide further conditions for the development of sports talent and the achievement of top results through funding development programs, preparation and competitions of top athletes. The involvement of socially sensitive groups in the sport system will be increased by co-financed sports programmes involving the groups mentioned because sport is one of the best ways for social engagement and the inclusion of each individual. ... the application of scientific knowledge in sport, to develop a proposal for a national model for the detection of sports talents. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] With insufficient funding, insufficiently outdated and poorly maintained sports infrastructure by stakeholders has been highlighted as one of the main disadvantages of sports in the Republic of Croatia, but the specific problem is the unregulated way of keeping records of sports infrastructure at all levels, and this area needs to be regulated by a new legislative framework. According to the Government of the Republic of Croatia's 2016-2020 Programme last year and this year, the Central State Sport Office co-financed the reconstruction of 24 sports facilities of interest for local sport throughout the Republic of Croatia. And the measures of the 4th general objective will allow for an even more systematic further investment in sport infrastructure, which requires the implementation of 3 specific objectives and measures of the 4th general objective, which is called to establish a systematic and rational approach to state sport infrastructure management, which will define the process of establishing and establishing a network of sustainable sports buildings and connect the network of sport buildings at local level into a single network of sports buildings at the level of the Republic of Croatia through the national information system in sport. The conditions for construction, maintenance and safety on sports buildings will be prescribed and standards and norms for the construction of sports buildings will be laid down. The need for new sports buildings as well as the restoration and additional equipment of existing foundations will be established, which will continue to invest further in sport infrastructure and will adapt the number and equipment of sports buildings to the real needs of sport, as well as the needs of disabled people to whom we will pay particular attention. Investment of central state infrastructure on the basis of criteria and a network of sports buildings will enable the progressive equalisation of the availability of content to their children in the Republic of Croatia. It will also encourage the construction of national sports centres in line with the needs of a particular sport or a group of sports. One of the key challenges of further development of sports is the quality of professional personnel in sport. The future development of sports expected in the Republic of Croatia is not possible without continued educational trainers as well as all other sports experts who must be able to monitor the development dictated by roof organisations, a multidisciplinary character of sport, as well as its economic impacts. In order to realise progress in the field of professional staff, 3 specific objectives and measures of the 5th general objective should be implemented, to improve the care of professional staff in sport, which will ensure the conditions for lifelong learning and training of professional staff, and to identify legal persons who will take care of the system of licensing in sport that will be introduced, and which has not existed in the Republic until today, and with the aim of improving the quality of training in sport, both through formal and informal and information education. We will equalise the provisions on professional staff for all sports, similar institutions and public documents in order to make their acquisitions available to all under equal conditions. We will conduct an analysis that will identify the real needs of sports for professional staff, and will, in accordance with the above, develop curricula and programmes of expert studies and staff training programmes that will... Greater quality of professional work, standards of interest and qualifications for professional staff in sport will be developed and installed in the Croatian qualification framework and directly into the European qualification framework. [[/The speaker doesn't understand.]] In this way, we will confirm the relevance, quality, visibility and comparability of qualifications in the EU area, and bring labour market needs into line. We will further encourage education, training, training, and the employment of expert staff. In order to minimise all shortcomings relating to the system of management in sport, and in particular the non-compliance and absence of official registrations and records in sport as clear provisions that would oblige stakeholders to keep records, thereby greatly reducing the possibility of quality further strategic planning and carrying out performance analysis, and setting clear targets based on accurate data in individual sports. Three specific objectives and actions and activities within the sixth general objective are defined to improve the system of governance in sport, and we will define more clearly the roles and tasks of individual stakeholders in the system of sport, promote gender equality, prevent conflicts at sports sites, encourage culture of non-violence, and volunteerism in sport. We will coordinate what is particularly important and connect public registers in sport, and implement the national information system in sport with the associated registers and reporting system that will greatly facilitate further strategic planning, strengthen the management of administrative capacities of stakeholders in the sport system, improve the efficiency of work in sports organisations, encourage greater application for EU funds, and encourage the creation of strategic actions. Furthermore, we will also enable sports activities to be carried out through crafts which the current law does not recognise. ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147422	The amendment is not accepted. The proposal for a national sport programme 2019 – In 2026, under the specific objective 1.1, the text states that with the aim of developing and encouraging the most successful and most massive sports in the Republic of Croatia, the basis of the categorisation of sports will determine sports of national interest. Furthermore, the text states that this will enable the creation of better quality conditions for further promotion and the achievement of top sports results. The text and the proposed measures make it clear that mass is one of the criteria for evaluation and categorisation, but it is also the goal. The measure 1.1.2. also provides for the development of an analysis of the situation and the adoption of a regulation on the criteria of categorisation in which the adoption will take into account that criterion, and a systematic discussion with stakeholders will be carried out for the making of that document. Since the proposal for an amendment is contained in the text, the amendment is not accepted as said.
