ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147427	We move to the following item of the agenda, which is: (a) Proposal for a decision on the resolution of the President, the Vice-President and the work of the members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures (a) Proposal for a Decision on the appointment of the President, the Vice-President and the work of the members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures, is proposed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia pursuant to Article 8. Law on the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures. The discussion was conducted by the committee responsible for the economy. I ask the representative of the motion, the respected economy ministry, entrepreneurship and craftsman Mr. Dark Horvat to explain the motion for a decision. Here you go, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-HR_2019-07-11-0.u147428	Dear Vice-President of the Croatian Parliament, esteemed MPs and MPs. The provision of Article 8 paragraph of the Law on the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures established that the President, Vice-President and other members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures appointed the Croatian Assembly on a proposal from the Government of the Republic of Croatia. The State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures is an independent and independent state body responsible for resolving complaints in public procurement procedures, concessions and selecting a private partner in public-private partnership projects and for submitting indictments for infringements by public procurement legislation. The State Commission is responsible for its work to the Croatian Assembly and consists of nine members, one of which is the president, two vice presidents and 6 members appointed to the term of office of 5 years with the possibility of another appointment. Before I begin to explain the proposal by the Government of the Republic of Croatia for the resolution, re-election and appointment of members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures, I would like to refer briefly to the public procurement system and its effects in the previous year. Public procurement has great economic potential and has a significant impact on economic growth. For example, through public procurement in the last year, the Republic spent over 46 billion kuna without VAT, almost 15% of total GDP, which makes up an increase of almost 11% compared to 2017 when 40.5 billion kuna or 13.4% of total GDP was spent in the same way. Nearly 2,000 EU-funded contracts were concluded last year in public procurement procedures, an increase of nearly 440% compared to the year before only 438 contracts were concluded. It is worth more than 7.1 billion kuna, therefore it is extremely important to know the legal framework, the principles of procurement and the rule of application of procedures. The greatest burden is certainly on those who directly participate in public procurement procedures. Public contracting authorities must be able to coordinate the public-procurement process between different bodies in the system and have to achieve the best value for money. However, public procurement procedures are very often specific and complex not only for public authorities but also for economic operators and especially for small and medium-sized companies. So today at this point focussed on the professionalization of the system. The available data from the 2018 statistical report published on the public procurement portal of the Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts are precisely showing that the new legal framework directs the system accordingly. For example, even though in most cases today large ones win public procurement procedures, in 2018, every other supply or supply bid almost 53%, in the public procurement process, came from micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, up by almost 50% compared to 2017. The European Commission is monitoring the progress of EU member states in public procurement, over 12 indicators, and I am pleased to point out that on the scale of the overall impact of public procurement and EU Member States, in the last 2 years of the application of the new Public Procurement Act, i.e. 2017 and 2018, from the unsatisfactory impact it had had in the previous 3 years raised to a satisfactory effect and placed itself on the side of countries such as Germany, the Netherlands, Belgium, Austria and Ireland. Last year, the State Commission marked 15 years of her work. I think we can say that the State Commission is a professional and effective appeal body with a high degree of transparency in its work. Today we have a highly digitized public procurement system that has one goal. Public procurement without paper from the planning phase through the conduct of the procedure, selection and to the appeal procedure and even the payment itself because a few days ago we created the obligation to make full use of the e-account. I would like to point out that persons involved in public procurement procedures must be professionals in their work. Since so far the President of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures, Mr Goran Matesic, Deputy President Nekica Vidic and members Alis Brand, Goran Bukvic and Dragica Markanovic, on 15 July 2019, the Government of the Republic of Croatia, held a meeting on 8 July 2019, established a proposal for a decision to resolve the president, deputy president and members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures. Furthermore, the same session also set out a proposal for a decision to appoint Mrs. Maji Kuhar, President of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures, Nelic Vidic, Deputy President and Mrs. Alis Brand, Zvonimir Jukic and Daniele Antolkovic to the members of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures with the beginning of the term of office on 16 July 2019. The main criteria led by the Government of the Republic of Croatia in adopting this proposal are professional integrity, expertise and proven experience in the field of public procurement, whether as a public contracting authority, a member of the body which decides on complaints or the experience gained in establishing a legal and institutional public procurement system. Let me briefly present the information on the professional qualifications of the proposed candidates. Maya Kuhar is a candidate for the President of the State Commission. - Mrs. Crescent. Maja Kuhar is a graduate lawyer with a legal exam. She has long-term work experience in the field of public procurement, which she first gained through the work of the State Commission as an adviser and then at the Ministry of Social Policy and Youth where she was, among other things, in charge of professional support in the combined procurement procedures for the needs of social care institutions. Since 2016, Mrs. The cook is employed in the Central State Office for Central Public Procurement in which he has served as Deputy Secretary of State since 2017. With her work so far, The cook has gained an enviable experience in preparing and implementing extremely complex public procurement procedures, experience in managing the organisation and teams of employees and has improved abroad, the Netherlands, England and America, respectively. - Mrs. Crescent. The cook ina certificate in the field of public procurement and is a longtime lecturer in public procurement education programmes. As an expert, she presents at international conferences and conferences and publishes expert works on public procurement as an author. - Mrs. Crescent. Nelica Vidic is a candidate for the deputy president of the State Commission. - Mrs. Crescent. Nelica Vidic is a master of international relations and diplomacy and degree. Economist, currently serving as deputy president of the State Commission. - Mrs. Crescent. For the purposes of Article 41(2), the applicant shall be an expert within the meaning of Article 41(2). The rules on education and the area of public procurement and on the possession of a valid certificate in the field of public procurement and shall be kept in the register of public procurement trainers at the Ministry of Economy, Entrepreneurship and crafts. - Mrs. Crescent. Vidic has a working experience of 30 years of which 20 years in the area of public procurement. Before taking office as deputy president of the State Commission, she gained experience in the management positions of the services of the KBC-Sister of Mercy and the FINA. She is the head of various projects in the field of public procurement, she participates in the projects of the FINA, the project of the cash centre, the project of the FINA office, the project of Brp.hr, she is the project manager of Spin, the project manager of the business process management project in the FINA and the project of joint procurement as a central public procurement body, i.e. she is the holder of 19 public-procurement categories for 36 health institutions. - Mrs. Crescent. Vidić is a lecturer at various professional workshops in the field of public procurement especially in the context of legal protection, a lecturer in the organization... the expert commission for the implementation of EU legislation, with the protection of the rights of the tenderers. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] - Mrs. Crescent. Vidic actively participated in the work of the State Commission not only as president of the council but also as a representative of the State Commission at meetings of the Council for the Prevention of Corruption, the working body of the Government of the Republic of Croatia and the National Council for Monitoring the implementation of the anti-corruption strategy. - Mrs. Crescent. Alice Brand, candidate for a member of the State Commission. - Mrs. Crescent. Alice Brand is a graduate. A lawyer who started working as an apprentice at the County Court in Zagreb immediately after the completion of her studies in 1989 is employed in HZMO where she worked until 2014 precisely on public procurement procedures and for all HZMO constituencies. In addition to the implementation of public procurement procedures, it also worked to prepare the draft contract for the supply of goods, works and services and to control the legal regularity of the contracts in question. - Mrs. Crescent. Brand is a specialist in the public procurement system and has a certificate in the field of public procurement slaughtered by an authorised coach in the public procurement system and has therefore implemented training and training of trainees related to public procurement activities and has made expert opinions and provided instructions regarding public procurement procedures, other HZMO building units, in particular to regional body units. It took part in the creation of a business policy of HZMO in the area of economic relations, particularly related to public procurement. - Mrs. Crescent. Brand was appointed a member of the State Commission in July 2014 where she performed her duty knowingly, motivation and above all professionally and professionally. Mr. Zvonimir Jukic is a member of the State Commission. Mr. Zvonimir Jukić is a graduate lawyer with a passed judicial exam, has a long-standing work experience in the field of public procurement, which he first gained through the Office for Public Procurement of the Government of the Republic of Croatia and the Department for Public Procurement of the Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts from 2006 to 2008 and later in the publishing of the National Newspapers. Mr. Jukić speaks English and German and has participated in various national and international teams that are intended to improve the Croatian and European public procurement systems, he has been a member of the implementation of the Public Procurement Directives in the Croatian legal system, he is a member of the project team for the development of an electronic advertiser for public procurement of the Republic of Croatia, as a Croatian representative of the European Commission's expert e-procurement group and is a member of the EU Official Journals Forums. He organised and moderated the number of consultations in the field of public procurement, civil, criminal, commercial law and took part as a lecturer in numerous national and international educations, conferences and expert conferences in the field of public procurement. Daniela Antolkovic is a candidate for a member of the State Commission. - Mrs. Crescent. Daniela Antolkovic is a graduate. a lawyer with a legal and public-booked examination. Since 2016, she has worked as Head of the Office for Resolution in Appeal Procedures at the State Commission, where she has acquired organisational skills through the management of the work of the service and has further perfected her skills. - Mrs. Crescent. Antolkovic has long-term public procurement experience, which was first gained through work at the Ministry of Health where she gained experience from the perspective of the contracting authorities in preparing and implementing public procurement procedures and then at the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures, working on the control of appeal cases, which contributed to obtaining a full picture in the public procurement segment. - Mrs. Crescent. Antolković completed a postgraduate specialist study at the University of Tora Vergat in Rome where she gained additional knowledge and experience through interdisciplinary approach as well as exchange of experiences with other colleagues from various countries and various legal areas, as well as a wider picture which also gave a contribution to the body in which they work. That study was fully scholarshipd by the European Bank for Reconstruction and Development. - Mrs. Crescent. Antolković has always shown a high level of expertise, motivation and independence in her work and has gained extensive knowledge and experience in the field of public procurement through her work so far. Allow me at the end of the short summary, i.e. the report on the work of the State Commission for the Control of Public Procurement Procedures in 2018. The State Commission has developed in the past 15 years both as an institution and as an authority in the field of public procurement in step with the entire system. Over the past 15 years, she has controlled over 18,000 items of overpriced value of procurement of more than 226 billion kunas. In its work, the State Commission has always sought to fulfil its competence with as little restrictions and extensions as possible of the duration of the procurement procedures. The importance of the State Commission is seen not only in the fact of the rapid and certain settlement of disputes in public procurement, but also in the fact that most public procurement practices are formed through decisions of the State Commission. In its work, the State Commission interprets and applies the right to public procurement in the light of European law and the practice of the European Court of Justice. At the same time, there is no need to ignore the contribution of members of the State Commission, the doctrine of public procurement rights through professional works, articles and participation in expert meetings. During 2018, 1170 complaints were received in the State Commission, compared to the total number of cases in the work before this state body, i.e. 1261, the average number of complaints in the work on a monthly basis is around 105 cases per month. Although a decrease in the number of complaints was recorded in 2014-2017, a continuous increase and complexity of appeal cases related to the possibility of using EU funds but with the increasing involvement of lawyers specialised in the procurement and participation of different consultants was observed. However, in 2018, there is an increase in the number of appeals cases by almost 25% compared to 2017, which is partly linked to the trust in the work of this institution by all interested entities in public procurement procedures and the effects of the application of the new Public Procurement Act. In particular, the public procurement law of 2016 entered into force on 1.1.2017 and only in 2018 after a certain degree of legal certainty had been established and after the time of verification of the procedures under official duty was opened, a significant number of complaints were also recorded. Despite the increase in the number of complaints in 2018, there was a reduction in the time limit within which the State's deadline to resolve complaints compared to previous years, so the average time from the receipt of the complaint to the decision of the State Commission is 34 days. It is time from completing the appeal case to making a decision this year is 14 days. In 2018, the high percentage trend of administrative courts confirmed the decisions of the State Commission was maintained. According to the 2018 data, courts have made their own decisions in only 7% of cases. In 7% of the decisions of the State Commission have been annulled and the case has been reinstated. Taking into account the decisions of the court to which the claim is rejected, the lawsuits are dismissed and the administrative court is suspending, the decisions of the State Commission have been confirmed in a high 86% of the cases. Transparency and public availability of the work of the State Commission is a mission that ensures both objectivity and predictability in the work of the State Commission. All decisions by the State Commission shall be published on the State Commission's website in full form, so with the name of the party and the application of the new Public Procurement Act have also enabled the publication of decisions by the High Administrative Court of the Republic of Croatia in disputes over public procurement. This has made the legal protection procedure predictable and transparent. The register of appeal cases is publicly available on the website of the State Commission and is updated in real time, which allows for a general condition in the movement of the case. As of 1 January 2018, it was possible to make an electronic complaint through interrelated systems of the State Commission and an electronic public procurement advertiser of the Republic of Croatia. After public publication, competition and supply of offers, electronic means of communication, the electronic complaint constitutes a further deadline until the full digitisation of the procurement procedures. The electronic complaint in public procurement is also a high achievement of the Republic of Croatia among other EU Member States. Therefore, we ask this home to adopt or make the proposal in question. Thank you very much.
